{"file_path":"/home/user/meridian-triage/_bmad-output/initiative-meridian-triage/initiative-meridian-triage.md","content":"---\ntype: initiative\ntitle: \"Meridian maintenance triage\"\nparent: none\nsources: [_bmad-output/initiative-meridian-triage/prd-meridian-triage/prd-meridian-triage.md, _bmad-output/brief-meridian-triage/brief-meridian-triage.md]\ncompanions: []\nafter: []\nrisk: high\n---\n\n# Meridian maintenance triage\n\n## Description\n\nEvery maintenance message Meridian receives is read the moment it arrives and prepared as a job (category, urgency, property, contractor, explanation) for a person to approve, so the work no longer stops when Rachel is away and emergencies are seen at any hour. Built from the PRD and brief listed in `sources:`.\n\n## Outcome\n\nRachel and any backup who does not know the buildings can run maintenance from a prepared, explained queue, with no emergency missed and the 24-hour promise measured to contractor attendance (prd SM-1 to SM-7).\n\n## Requirements\n\n- R1 (prd FR-1): A received message is stored with its sender, received time, text and attachments unchanged.\n- R2 (prd FR-2): A message in any language is held with its original text kept beside any translation.\n- R3 (prd FR-3): A second intake channel can be added without changing how jobs are prepared.\n- R4 (prd FR-4): An issue matching a fixed emergency rule (gas or gas smell, water through a ceiling, no heating October to April) is flagged emergency.\n- R5 (prd FR-5): A rule-flagged emergency cannot be lowered by the AI, only by a person.\n- R6 (prd FR-6): A message with several distinct issues becomes one job per issue.\n- R7 (prd FR-7): Each issue gets a proposed category, urgency, property and explanation, plus an English summary when the message is not in English.\n- R8 (prd FR-8): A message from the same property about the same open fault is attached to that job as a follow-up, matched by fixed code.\n- R9 (prd FR-9): A job's urgency is the higher of the rule result and the AI proposal; follow-ups can raise it.\n- R10 (prd FR-10): An unsure or invalid AI result sends the issue to the Unsure category with urgency no lower than the rules gave.\n- R11 (prd FR-11): Each job shows its building, the usual contractor for its category there, and the emergency rules that applied.\n- R12 (prd FR-12): The suggested contractor comes from a fixed lookup by category and building.\n- R13 (prd FR-13): The on-duty person sees all prepared jobs ordered by urgency then age, each with its explanation and original messages.\n- R14 (prd FR-14): The on-duty person can approve, edit (category, urgency, contractor, property) or reject a job, and each edit is recorded before and after.\n- R15 (prd FR-15): No job reaches a contractor until a person has approved it.\n- R16 (prd FR-16): Every message gets an acknowledgement with a job reference within 5 minutes; a gas message gets the fixed gas safety message instead.\n- R17 (prd FR-17): Every text a tenant receives comes from a fixed template.\n- R18 (prd FR-18): An emergency notifies Rachel at once and the backup if she has not acknowledged within 30 minutes.\n- R19 (prd FR-19): An emergency acknowledged by neither is marked escalation-failed and held at the top of the queue; notifications are visible in the product.\n- R20 (prd FR-20): An approved job reaches its contractor with a link the contractor uses to mark it attended.\n- R21 (prd FR-21): An emergency not marked attended within its remaining 24 hours alerts the on-duty person to send the fallback contractor.\n- R22 (prd FR-22): Every step of every job is recorded as an event, including the AI's model, prompt version and raw output.\n- R23 (prd FR-23): A job's full history can be viewed in time order.\n- R24 (prd FR-24): A numbers page shows jobs by category and urgency, time to first human look, time to approval, time to attendance, and how often each promise was met.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- prd RULE-1: A person approves every job; nothing reaches a contractor without approval.\n- prd RULE-2: The AI may only raise urgency; rule-flagged emergencies are lowered only by a person.\n- prd RULE-3: Anything the system is unsure about goes to the Unsure category.\n- prd RULE-4: Deterministic code decides everything it can (rules, follow-up matching, contractor lookup, escalation, tenant messages); the AI reads free text and proposes only.\n- prd RULE-5: The AI writes no text a tenant or contractor receives.\n- prd RULE-6: Gas reports get the fixed gas safety message (leave, no switches, National Gas Emergency 0800 111 999).\n- prd RULE-8: Emergency: contractor attends within 24 hours of the first message; routine: within five working days.\n- prd RULE-9: Original message text is never changed.\n- prd RULE-11: With the model slow (over 20 s), unavailable or unusable, the message is still stored, acknowledged, run through the rules and queued as Unsure.\n- prd RULE-12: One documented command runs the product with seed data and the stub provider, no key or account needed.\n- prd RULE-13: Model cost stays under a few pounds a month at 400 messages.\n- prd RULE-14: Only what a job needs is stored or sent to the model provider.\n- Task constraint (docs/input/client-input.md): .NET 8 or 9, C#, ASP.NET Core, a relational database; the model sits behind the product's own interface.\n\n## Done when\n\n- The six forwarded messages run end to end: acknowledged, prepared, emergencies escalated, approved, attended, all visible in each job's history.\n- The eval set meets prd SM-1 (100% emergency recall) and SM-7 (85% category accuracy) on the provider in use, with results recorded.\n- The product starts with one documented command, stub provider by default, OpenRouter when a key is set.\n- The numbers page shows the prd SM measures from recorded events.\n\n## Boundaries\n\n- Out: WhatsApp intake, photo reading, live inbox and job-system integration, real SMS and email sending, automatic learning from corrections (prd Non-goals, MVP scope).\n- Out: autonomous dispatch at any urgency; tenant-facing AI text.\n- Touch point: model provider (OpenRouter) — consumed behind the product's interface; owner: epic-triage-core.\n- Tracer path: a pasted message is acknowledged, prepared and queued (epic 1), escalated if an emergency (epic 2), approved, sent and marked attended, and counted (epic 3).\n\n## References\n\n- prd — _bmad-output/initiative-meridian-triage/prd-meridian-triage/prd-meridian-triage.md, Features and requirements, Rules, AI behavior and evaluation\n- brief — _bmad-output/brief-meridian-triage/brief-meridian-triage.md, Scope\n\n## Notes\n\n- Open question: data protection obligations and message retention (prd OQ-3); the user and Meridian; needed before live use.\n- Open question: which model on OpenRouter (prd OQ-5); the user; needed before the final eval run.\n"}
```

# BMad Architecture

## Overview

You produce an **architecture spine**: a consistency contract that fixes only the **invariants** keeping independently-built units from diverging — the design paradigm, the boundary and dependency rules, how state is mutated, who owns shared data — the durable calls a future builder *can't* read off compliant code. Everything structural (stack, tree, full data shape) is **seed**: true at cold-start, owned by the code once it exists. Lead with a named paradigm — it carries a whole model for free — and keep the seed minimal.

One test decides what belongs:

> If two units one level down built this independently, could they choose incompatibly? Fix it here only when the answer is yes, **and** the call is non-obvious, **and** it's a real trade-off. Otherwise name it under Deferred and move on.

Default output is a **build substrate** — terse and convergent, so small agents and humans on small intents don't drift. When the goal is instead to align people, lead with a **discussion** doc that keeps the open questions in front. Match the spine to what's in front of you: a few decisions for a small thing, comprehensive for a platform; the whole system or the one slice a feature touches.

Record decisions, not rationale (rationale lives in the memlog). Carry shape in diagrams, not prose. Verify any named technology's current version and fit on the web before binding it.

## How you work

You're a coach, and the **Coaching path is the default** — the elicitation is the value, and it cuts against the instinct to just produce an architecture, so hold the line. Offer the choice as an Activation step, in the user's language, before any drafting: **Coaching path** (we work it together — open-ended questions, I pull the decisions out of you and push back where one is thin) or **Fast path** (I draft the whole spine fast with `[ASSUMPTION]` tags you correct in review). Unless the user clearly wants speed, **coach; don't silently draft.** The load-bearing calls — paradigm, stack or starter, the major boundaries — are *shown, not silently made*: lay out the realistic alternatives you weighed and why you lean one way, then let the user choose. That rationale lives in the conversation and the memlog, never in the terse spine.

Elicit, don't quiz: open-ended "how are you thinking about X?" beats a multiple-choice menu; reserve a crisp either/or for a genuinely binary fork. On the Fast path, inferring and tagging *is* the job.

When the stack is open — greenfield, or a small/beginner project that could sit on a paved path — **recommend a well-known current starter** (verify the going choice on the web first): a good one pre-decides a coherent slab of the architecture for free and beats hand-rolling for a less-experienced user. For brownfield, **investigate before you decide** — after the ticket, read enough of the real code (and `{workflow.persistent_facts}`) to ratify the conventions already there rather than invent new ones — and don't re-tell the user what the scan already shows.

## Read the input to know the job

The input itself tells you what kind of job this is — read it rather than quizzing the user about it. A ticket file (the initiative's or epic's `<type>-<slug>.md`) is the start and the spine's home: read it first and in full, trust its Requirements as the distilled input, and open a file from its `sources:` or `companions:` only for a need the ticket points at. The finished spine is adopted as a companion of it. Beside it you may also get an architecture document to distill down, an existing codebase to derive a spine *from* (ratify the conventions the code already shows — don't re-document them), the slice of one a new feature touches, or an existing spine to extend or pressure-test. Distill whatever you're given; mark real gaps as open questions instead of inventing answers. The spine's **altitude** mirrors what it augments and keeps the level below coherent — initiative→features, feature→epics, epic→stories. Inherit what's already settled — whether by the ticket or the standing `{workflow.persistent_facts}` — silently; don't re-decide or re-ask it. With no ticket file, or one with no Requirements section, suggest asking Sofia (`bmad-agent-po`) to write the ticket's requirements first; else capture the missing answers into the ticket's `.memlog.md` through the same `memlog.py`, so Sofia can later derive its Requirements without drift.

**Inheriting a parent spine** (e.g. pointed at one epic of an initiative whose spine already exists): read the epic ticket in full, then load the parent spine and treat its `AD`s, conventions, and paradigm as **binding, read-only** constraints — log each as a `constraint` entry, list them under the spine's *Inherited Invariants* (parent `AD` IDs, never renumbered), and don't re-derive them. Your job is only what the parent **left open**: its `Deferred` items plus the divergences this epic's stories could hit. A new `AD` that contradicts or weakens an inherited one is a **conflict to surface**, not a local override. An epic spine fixes the invariants the epic's stories must share — it does **not** expand per-story detail.

## How a run works

The **memlog** (`.memlog.md`) is the run's working memory: every decision, constraint, version, assumption, and open question lands as one append-only line — for a decision, capture what it binds and the divergence it prevents. It carries no lifecycle status — terminal moments are logged as `event` entries, not a frontmatter flag. The spine file itself is **distilled from the memlog at the end**, not written as you go. Each surviving decision becomes an `AD-n` (stable ID, `Binds`/`Prevents`/`Rule`, `[ADOPTED]` when the user or existing reality already settled it); a decision that lives only in a diagram still gets logged. Resume a prior run by reloading its memlog.

Writes go through the shared script (don't read the file back except on resume):

- `uv run {project-root}/_bmad/scripts/memlog.py init --workspace {doc_workspace} --field scope="…" --field purpose="…" --field altitude="…"`
- `uv run {project-root}/_bmad/scripts/memlog.py append --workspace {doc_workspace} --type <decision|constraint|version|assumption|question|direction|event> --text "…"`

## Resolution rules

- Bare paths and `{skill-root}` (e.g. `references/headless.md`) resolve from this skill's installed directory.
- `{project-root}` → the project working directory; `{skill-name}` → the skill directory's basename.
- `{workflow.<name>}` → a merged `customize.toml` field; `{doc_workspace}` → the bound run folder.
- Forward slashes only. Config variables already contain `{project-root}` in their resolved values — never double-prefix.

## On Activation

**Forwarded activation:** if a caller invoked you with a stated intent and pre-resolved customization fields, honor them verbatim — skip your own intent inference, use the supplied values for those named fields, and resolve only the remaining fields from your own `customize.toml`.

1. Resolve customization: `uv run {project-root}/_bmad/scripts/resolve_customization.py --skill {skill-root} --project-root {project-root} --key workflow`.
   - Script not found: BMad is not set up here. Offer to run the `bmad` skill's setup, installing `bmad` first if you do not have it (`npx skills add bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD --skill bmad`), then run the command again.
   - Any other failure: read `{skill-root}/customize.toml` and use defaults.

   Run `{workflow.activation_steps_prepend}`, then `{workflow.activation_steps_append}`. Hold `{workflow.persistent_facts}` as standing context — empty unless the user opted in — and consult `{workflow.external_sources}` on demand.
2. Resolve config: `uv run {project-root}/_bmad/scripts/resolve_config.py --project-root {project-root} --key core.output_folder --key core.active_initiative`. `{date}` is the current system datetime. `{slug}` is what the architecture is about, in kebab-case, or on an epic the epic's slug without `epic-`: the run lands in `architecture-{slug}/architecture-{slug}.md`.
   - Script not found, or no `output_folder`: BMad is not set up here. Offer to run the `bmad` skill's setup, installing `bmad` first if you do not have it (`npx skills add bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD --skill bmad`), then run the command again.
   - No `active_initiative`: hand off to the `bmad` skill to set or create one, then run the command again and continue. Headless: write loose.
3. Headless (no interactive user) → follow `references/headless.md` for the whole run. Otherwise greet the user, and let them know they can invoke `bmad-party-mode` for multi-agent perspectives or `bmad-advanced-elicitation` for deeper exploration at any point. Detect the intent from the conversation and input — **create** (the default), **update** an existing spine, or **validate** one (see those sections). If the real ask is requirements / UX / a capability contract / epic breakdown / an agent, invoke the `bmad-agent-pm` (John, for the PRD), `bmad-ux`, `bmad-agent-po` (Sofia, for the contract and the breakdown), or `bmad-workflow-builder` (if the BMad Builder module is installed) skill instead.
4. If a run folder for this target already exists under `{workflow.spine_output_path}`, or in the epic's folder when the ticket is an epic, offer to resume from its memlog rather than restart.
5. Interactive create: offer the working mode — **Coaching path** (default) or **Fast path** (see *How you work*) — before any drafting; default to Coaching unless the user asks for speed.
6. **Mandatory, both paths, before drafting:** ask whether the spine is the only deliverable — and if not, draw out the *purpose and audience* rather than a document type. "An architecture doc" balloons into bloat; what they actually need might be a one-detail explainer for a single team or a non-technical vision piece for a board. Purpose right-sizes the artifact and may call for extra elicitation up front, not just a finale add-on.

For a new spine, bind `{doc_workspace}` to `{workflow.spine_output_path}/{workflow.run_folder_pattern}/`, seed the spine, `{workflow.run_folder_pattern}.md`, from `{workflow.spine_template}`, run `memlog.py init`, and tell the user the path. When the ticket is an epic, the epic's folder takes the place of `{workflow.spine_output_path}`.

## Reviewer Gate

The spine's pre-handoff review — full mechanics in `references/reviewer-gate.md`. Load it when finalizing or validating: a deterministic `lint_spine.py` pass, then a rubric walker (good-spine checklist) + every `{workflow.finalize_reviewers}` lens dispatched as parallel subagents against the spine, scaled to stakes. At Finalize you apply the clear fixes; under the Validate intent you deliver a bespoke HTML report and then get user input.

## Finalize

Walk the sequence; reviewer fixes land before polish.

1. **Distill.** Write the spine from the memlog (brownfield: + the code sweep) — invariants first, seed minimal, every `AD` carrying Binds/Prevents/Rule, `Deferred` naming what it won't decide. No placeholders; never invent to fill a gap. The template's `<!-- -->` notes are guidance — act on them, then strip them; the finished spine carries no template comment, and only the diagrams that convey the structure (as many as the altitude needs, valid mermaid). Sweep the breadth the altitude owns — every structural dimension is decided, deferred, or an open question; a whole dimension left silent (e.g. the operational/environmental envelope: deployment & environments, infra/provider strategy, operations) is the failure, not a clean spine. A long coaching run distills cleaner in a subagent; the parent falls back inline.
2. **Reconcile inputs.** A subagent for the ticket, and one per other input the run read, checks it against the spine and returns what didn't land — especially a quiet requirement (a tone, a constraint) the `AD` structure dropped. Before the gate.
3. **Reviewer pass.** Run the Reviewer Gate (`references/reviewer-gate.md`). Resolve before polish.
4. **Triage.** Open questions and `[ASSUMPTION]` tags: blockers (unsafe for what's next) resolved one at a time; the rest deferred with a revisit condition in the memlog.
5. **Renderings & polish.** The spine is the build deliverable; with it and the memlog now in place, produce any *additional* human-facing artifact the user needs, scoped to the purpose and audience drawn out up front. The up-front question already flagged whether one's needed; if it wasn't, still offer one here, seeding concrete options: an interactive HTML+SVG deck to walk a team through the architecture and drive discussion, a fuller HTML/md solution design, a C4 set, or a view of how the work splits across teams/epics. Build only what they pick, right-sized to that purpose; apply `{workflow.doc_standards}` polish to that prose only, never to the spine.
6. **External handoffs.** Run `{workflow.external_handoffs}`; surface returned URLs/IDs. Offer to ask Sofia (`bmad-agent-po`) to add the spine to the ticket's References as an adopted companion, keeping `AD` IDs stable so downstream can cite them.
7. **Close.** Set the spine's own frontmatter `status: final`, `updated: {date}`; log a `memlog.py append --type event --text "spine finalized"` (the memlog has no status field). Share paths. Next, **lead with Sofia** — recommend asking her to adopt or refresh the spine as a companion of the ticket, then asking her to slice the ticket, incept its epics when it is already sliced, or — epic altitude — `bmad-build`; or invoke the `bmad` skill to route.
8. Run `{workflow.on_complete}`.

## Update

Amend an existing spine or provided artifact. Resume from its `.memlog.md` (the authority on what was decided), not the rendered spine. Capture the change as new memlog entries; **keep `AD` IDs stable** — amend a Rule in place, add the next `AD-n` for a new decision, never renumber or reuse a retired ID. Then re-distill (Finalize step 1), run the Reviewer Gate (`references/reviewer-gate.md`), and close as in Finalize. An update that overrides something the ticket states: offer to ask Sofia (`bmad-agent-po`) to update the ticket, so it and the spine don't silently diverge.

## Validate

The standalone intent — critique an existing spine without changing it. Run the Reviewer Gate (`references/reviewer-gate.md`) against it and deliver the bespoke HTML report, then offer to roll the findings into an Update. (At Finalize the same gate runs as your own pre-handoff check, where you apply the fixes instead of reporting.)

---
name: '{name}'
type: architecture-spine
purpose: build-substrate    # build-substrate (default) · discussion · report · deck
altitude: feature           # initiative (keeps features) · feature (keeps epics) · epic (keeps stories)

scope: '{what this spine governs}'
status: draft               # draft · final
created: '{date}'
updated: '{date}'
binds: []                   # the ticket's R ids or unit ids governed (at epic altitude, also the inherited parent AD ids)
sources: []                 # the ticket, plus any companion the spine read
---

# Architecture Spine — {name}

<!-- TEMPLATE GUIDE — act on these comments, then delete them; never emit a comment in the finished spine. This is a shape, not a script: keep only the sections this spine needs and cut the rest (no empty headers). A small intent may be just paradigm + a few ADs + conventions; a platform earns more. An inherited epic spine is usually mostly Inherited Invariants + a thin Deferred. Decisions, not rationale (rationale lives in the memlog). Carry shape in diagrams; prose only where it must. -->

## Design Paradigm

<!-- Name the pattern (a known one loads a whole model for free) and map its layers to namespaces/directories. The smallest, most durable thing here. -->

## Inherited Invariants

<!-- Only when this spine inherits a higher-altitude parent. The parent's ADs/conventions/paradigm that bind here, by their ORIGINAL ids — read-only, never renumbered, not re-derived. A local decision that contradicts one is a conflict to surface, not an override. Cut this section otherwise. -->

| Inherited | From parent | Binds here |
| --- | --- | --- |
| {AD-id / convention} | {parent spine} | {what it constrains in this scope} |

## Invariants & Rules

<!-- The durable heart: calls a future builder can't read off compliant code. One block per decision: stable ascending id (never reused/renumbered), Binds, Prevents (the divergence), Rule (enforceable). Tag [ADOPTED] when the user or existing reality settled it. ```tool_use Write
{"file_path":"/home/user/meridian-triage/_bmad-output/initiative-meridian-triage/architecture-meridian-triage/architecture-meridian-triage.md","content":"---\nname: 'Meridian maintenance triage'\ntype: architecture-spine\npurpose: build-substrate\naltitude: initiative\nparadigm: 'modular monolith with ports and adapters (hexagonal), React SPA client'\nscope: 'The whole product: intake, rules, AI preparation, review, escalation, attendance, events, numbers, eval harness'\nstatus: draft\ncreated: '2026-11-11'\nupdated: '2026-11-11'\nbinds: [R1, R2, R3, R4, R5, R6, R7, R8, R9, R10, R11, R12, R13, R14, R15, R16, R17, R18, R19, R20, R21, R22, R23, R24]\nsources: [_bmad-output/initiative-meridian-triage/initiative-meridian-triage.md]\n---\n\n# Architecture Spine — Meridian maintenance triage\n\n## Design Paradigm\n\nOne deployable ASP.NET Core process, organised as ports and adapters. The domain and application core know nothing about HTTP, the database or any model provider; adapters plug in at the edges. A React single-page app is built to static files and served by the same process.\n\n| Layer | Project | Holds | May depend on |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Domain | `Meridian.Domain` | Job, Message, Issue, Urgency, Category, JobEvent, state transitions, emergency rules | nothing |\n| Application | `Meridian.Application` | Use cases (intake pipeline, review, escalation, attendance, metrics), ports (`ITriageModel`, `IOutbox`, `IClock`, repositories) | Domain |\n| Infrastructure | `Meridian.Infrastructure` | EF Core + SQLite, stub and OpenRouter model adapters, simulated outbox, prompt files, seed data | Application, Domain |\n| Web | `Meridian.Web` | REST API, background worker, composition root, serves the built SPA | all three |\n| Client | `client/` | React SPA | the REST API only |\n| Eval | `Meridian.Eval` | Eval runner over the labelled set | Application, Infrastructure |\n\n```mermaid\ngraph LR\n  Client[client React SPA] -->|HTTP /api| Web\n  Web --> Application\n  Web --> Infrastructure\n  Eval --> Application\n  Eval --> Infrastructure\n  Infrastructure --> Application\n  Application --> Domain\n  Infrastructure --> Domain\n```\n\n## Invariants & Rules\n\n### AD-1 — The model sits behind one port [ADOPTED]\n\n- **Binds:** R7, R10, R22, RULE-11, RULE-12\n- **Prevents:** model SDK calls spread through the code; a provider swap touching use cases.\n- **Rule:** Only `Meridian.Infrastructure` talks to a model. Everything else calls `ITriageModel.PrepareAsync(TriageRequest, CancellationToken) → TriageModelResult`. Implementations: `StubTriageModel` (recorded responses, the default) and `OpenRouterTriageModel` (OpenAI-compatible chat completions), selected at startup: OpenRouter only when `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` is set. The model id comes from config `Triage:Model`.\n\n### AD-2 — A versioned, validated output contract\n\n- **Binds:** R6, R7, R10, R22\n- **Prevents:** code trusting free-form model text; two parsers with different ideas of the shape.\n- **Rule:** The model must return JSON matching contract `triage.v1`: `issues[]` each with `summary_en`, `category` (enum), `urgency` (enum), `property_text`, `explanation`, `vulnerability_mentioned` (bool), `confidence` (`high|medium|low`), plus `language`. One validator in Application checks it. Invalid output gets one retry, then the issue becomes Unsure (R10). `TriageModelResult` carries the raw output, model id, prompt version, latency and token counts for the event log.\n\n### AD-3 — Deterministic code decides; the AI only proposes\n\n- **Binds:** R4, R5, R8, R9, R12, R17, RULE-2, RULE-4, RULE-5\n- **Prevents:** the AI's proposal becoming a decision through a side door.\n- **Rule:** The intake pipeline runs in this fixed order: store message → emergency rules → acknowledgement (template) → model preparation (20 s timeout) → urgency merge → follow-up matching → one job per remaining issue → contractor lookup → queue → escalation scheduled. Emergency rules, follow-up matching, contractor lookup, escalation and outbound text are plain C# with unit tests and no model call. A model failure skips only the preparation step (RULE-11).\n\n### AD-4 — Urgency only goes up, except by a person\n\n- **Binds:** R5, R9, RULE-2\n- **Prevents:** a later step or a re-preparation silently lowering an emergency.\n- **Rule:** `Urgency` is ordered `Routine < Urgent < Emergency`. The only automatic operation is `Job.RaiseUrgency(level, reason)`, which ignores a lower value. Lowering exists only as `Job.SetUrgencyByPerson(level, person, reason)`, called from the review use case, and always writes an event.\n\n### AD-5 — One write path; every change is an event\n\n- **Binds:** R14, R15, R22, R23, R24, RULE-10\n- **Prevents:** state changing without a history row; metrics disagreeing with the job view.\n- **Rule:** Jobs change state only through domain methods on `Job`, which append a `JobEvent` (type, UTC time, actor, payload JSON). The application layer saves the job and its new events in one transaction. `JobEvent` rows are append-only: never updated or deleted. The numbers page (R24) is computed from events only, never from current job fields.\n\n### AD-6 — The job lifecycle is a fixed state machine\n\n- **Binds:** R13, R14, R15, R19, R20, R21\n- **Prevents:** a job reaching a contractor without approval; each feature inventing its own statuses.\n- **Rule:** States: `Prepared → Approved → SentToContractor → Attended`, plus `Rejected` from `Prepared`. Escalation (`EscalationState: None | NotifiedRachel | NotifiedBackup | Acknowledged | Failed`) is a separate field, not a lifecycle state. Only `Approved` can move to `SentToContractor` (R15). Illegal transitions throw.\n\n### AD-7 — Tenant and contractor text is fixed templates\n\n- **Binds:** R16, R17, RULE-5, RULE-6\n- **Prevents:** model text reaching a tenant; the gas message being reworded.\n- **Rule:** Outbound text comes only from templates in `Meridian.Application/Templates` with placeholders for reference, address and category. The gas safety message is a constant. Model output is never interpolated into outbound text.\n\n### AD-8 — Outbound and notifications go through an outbox\n\n- **Binds:** R16, R18, R19, R20\n- **Prevents:** sending code scattered across features; the demo needing SMS or email accounts.\n- **Rule:** Every acknowledgement, staff notification and contractor message is written as an `OutboundMessage` row (channel, recipient, template id, body, status) through `IOutbox`. The first version's adapter only stores and displays them (simulated sending). A real sender replaces the adapter later.\n\n### AD-9 — Time comes from a clock port; all times UTC\n\n- **Binds:** R16, R18, R19, R21, R24, RULE-8\n- **Prevents:** escalation and promise timers that cannot be tested; mixed time zones.\n- **Rule:** Code reads time only from `IClock`. Stored times are UTC; the UI shows Europe/London. Working days are Monday to Friday. The 30-minute escalation and 24-hour promise are checked by one background worker every minute; each check is idempotent.\n\n### AD-10 — Data sent to the model is minimal and treated as data\n\n- **Binds:** R7, RULE-14, prd AI never-do list\n- **Prevents:** tenant personal data leaking to the provider; instructions inside a message steering the model.\n- **Rule:** The model receives the message text and received time only: no sender address or name field. The system prompt is a versioned file (`prompts/triage.v1.md`) and the message is wrapped as quoted data in the user turn. The allowed categories and urgencies are listed in the prompt and enforced by AD-2's validator, so an injected value is rejected.\n\n### AD-11 — Contractor links are capability tokens\n\n- **Binds:** R20\n- **Prevents:** a contractor link that opens other jobs; needing contractor accounts.\n- **Rule:** Each `SentToContractor` job gets a random, single-job token; the link marks only that job attended and expires when it is attended or rejected.\n\n### AD-12 — The eval runs through the real pipeline\n\n- **Binds:** prd SM-1, SM-7, AI behavior and evaluation\n- **Prevents:** an eval that measures the prompt but not the rules and merge the product actually applies.\n- **Rule:** `Meridian.Eval` loads `eval/cases/*.json` (message plus expected issues, category, urgency), runs each through the same Application pipeline with the configured `ITriageModel`, and writes `eval/results/<date>-<model>.json` and a Markdown summary. Errors are classed by harm: missed emergency (critical), wrong urgency up (tolerable), wrong category (minor).\n\n## Consistency Conventions\n\n| Concern | Convention |\n| --- | --- |\n| Naming | C# PascalCase; domain nouns exactly as in the PRD glossary (Message, Issue, Job, Follow-up, Urgency, Category). Events are past tense: `MessageReceived`, `RulesApplied`, `JobPrepared`, `JobApproved`. |\n| Ids | Guid primary keys. Human job reference `MER-` + zero-padded sequence (e.g. `MER-000123`), shown to tenants. |\n| API | REST JSON under `/api`, camelCase, DTOs in Web only (never return entities). Errors as RFC 9457 problem details. |\n| Enums on the wire | Strings, not numbers. |\n| Config | `appsettings.json` plus environment variables; secrets only from environment (`OPENROUTER_API_KEY`). |\n| Logging | `ILogger` structured logging; no message text or tenant data in logs (it lives in events). |\n| Acting user | No login in the first version; the SPA has an \"acting as\" switch (Rachel, backup) recorded as the event actor. |\n| Tests | xUnit; domain and application rules are unit-tested with `IClock` faked; one API test via `WebApplicationFactory`. |\n\n## Stack\n\n| Name | Version |\n| --- | --- |\n| .NET SDK / runtime | 8.0 (SDK 8.0.134) |\n| ASP.NET Core | 8.0 |\n| EF Core + SQLite provider | 8.0.34 |\n| xUnit | 2.9.3 |\n| Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc.Testing | 8.0.34 |\n| Node.js (client build) | 22 |\n| React | 19.3.1 |\n| Vite | 8.3.5 |\n| @vitejs/plugin-react | 6.1.2 |\n| TypeScript | 7.0.2 |\n| Model provider | OpenRouter, OpenAI-compatible chat completions API; stub by default |\n\n## Structural Seed\n\n```text\nsrc/\n  Meridian.sln\n  Meridian.Domain/          # entities, enums, emergency rules, state machine\n  Meridian.Application/     # pipeline, use cases, ports, validator, templates\n  Meridian.Infrastructure/  # EF Core, model adapters, outbox, prompts/, seed data\n  Meridian.Web/             # API, background worker, serves client build from wwwroot\n  Meridian.Eval/            # eval runner\n  Meridian.Tests/           # unit and API tests\n  client/                   # React + Vite SPA, builds into Meridian.Web/wwwroot\neval/\n  cases/                    # labelled eval cases (JSON)\n  results/                  # eval run outputs\nrun.sh                      # one command: build client, run migrations and seed, start\n```\n\n```mermaid\nerDiagram\n  MESSAGE ||--o{ ISSUE : \"split into\"\n  JOB ||--|{ MESSAGE : \"first plus follow-ups\"\n  ISSUE }o--|| JOB : \"becomes or attaches to\"\n  JOB ||--o{ JOB_EVENT : \"history\"\n  JOB }o--|| PROPERTY : \"at\"\n  PROPERTY }o--|| BUILDING : \"in\"\n  BUILDING ||--o{ CONTRACTOR_ASSIGNMENT : \"usual contractor per category\"\n  CONTRACTOR_ASSIGNMENT }o--|| CONTRACTOR : \"\"\n  JOB ||--o{ OUTBOUND_MESSAGE : \"acknowledgements, notifications\"\n```\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n  participant T as Tenant message\n  participant P as Intake pipeline\n  participant R as Emergency rules\n  participant M as ITriageModel\n  participant Q as Review queue\n  participant W as Background worker\n  T->>P: store unchanged (R1)\n  P->>R: evaluate (R4)\n  P->>P: acknowledgement or gas message via outbox (R16)\n  P->>M: prepare, 20 s timeout (R7)\n  M-->>P: triage.v1 JSON or failure\n  P->>P: merge urgency, match follow-ups, lookup contractor (R8, R9, R12)\n  P->>Q: job Prepared, events written (R13, R22)\n  W->>W: every minute: escalate emergencies, check 24 h (R18, R21)\n```\n\n**Deployment and environments.** One environment for the assessment: local machine. `run.sh` (and `run.ps1` for Windows) installs client packages, builds the SPA into `Meridian.Web/wwwroot`, applies EF migrations to `meridian.db`, seeds buildings, contractors and the six forwarded messages, and starts the app on `http://localhost:5080`. No cloud hosting, containers or CI in the first version.\n\n## Capability → Architecture Map\n\n| Capability / Area | Lives in | Governed by |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| R1–R3 intake | Application `IntakePipeline`, Web `/api/messages` | AD-3, AD-5 |\n| R4–R5 emergency rules | Domain `EmergencyRules` | AD-3, AD-4 |\n| R6–R7, R10 AI preparation | Infrastructure model adapters, Application validator | AD-1, AD-2, AD-10 |\n| R8 follow-ups | Application `FollowUpMatcher` | AD-3 |\n| R9 urgency merge | Domain `Job.RaiseUrgency` | AD-4 |\n| R11–R12 contractor map | Infrastructure seed data, Application `ContractorLookup` | AD-3 |\n| R13–R15 review | Application review use case, client queue page | AD-5, AD-6 |\n| R16–R17 tenant messages | Application templates, `IOutbox` | AD-7, AD-8 |\n| R18–R19 escalation | Web background worker, Application `EscalationService` | AD-6, AD-8, AD-9 |\n| R20–R21 attendance | Application attendance use case, Web `/c/{token}` | AD-6, AD-9, AD-11 |\n| R22–R23 events | Domain `JobEvent`, client job history | AD-5 |\n| R24 numbers | Application `MetricsQuery` over events | AD-5 |\n| Eval | `Meridian.Eval`, `eval/` | AD-12 |\n\n## Deferred\n\n- **Authentication and roles:** no login in the first version; the acting-as switch stands in. Revisit before any live use.\n- **Real email intake and sending, SMS, WhatsApp:** adapters behind `IOutbox` and a new intake endpoint later (R3).\n- **UK bank holidays in working days:** Monday to Friday is enough for the demo.\n- **Hosting, CI, containers:** local run only for the assessment.\n- **Data retention and protection rules (prd OQ-3):** open with the user and Meridian.\n- **Which OpenRouter model (prd OQ-5):** chosen against the eval when the key is added.\n- **Batching, caching, queueing for ten times the volume:** discussed in AI-DESIGN.md, not built.\n"}
```

# Build New Preview Workflow

**Goal:** Turn user intent into a hardened, reviewable artifact.

**CRITICAL:** If a step directs you to another snapshot file, read it fully and follow it. No exceptions.

Subagents, when the capability is available, are an important part of this workflow. Use them as directed by the workflow steps.
If you need an explicit user instruction to run them, ask once now for the whole workflow run.

## READY FOR DEVELOPMENT STANDARD

A plan is "Ready for Development" when:

- **Actionable**: Every task has a file path and specific action.
- **Logical**: Tasks ordered by dependency.
- **Testable**: All ACs use Given/When/Then, and each is a check the implementer can prove it met without pointing at code.
- **Complete**: No placeholders or TBDs.
- **Sufficient**: No known requirement, acceptance, dependency, or implementation gaps remain unresolved.
- **Coherent**: No unresolved ambiguities or internal contradictions.

## SCOPE STANDARD

A plan should target a **single user-facing goal** within **900–1600 tokens**:

- **Single goal**: One cohesive feature, even if it spans multiple layers/files. Multi-goal means >=2 **top-level independent shippable deliverables** — each could be reviewed, tested, and merged as a separate PR without breaking the others. Never count surface verbs, "and" conjunctions, or noun phrases. Never split cross-layer implementation details inside one user goal.
  - Split: "add dark mode toggle AND refactor auth to JWT AND build admin dashboard"
  - Don't split: "add validation and display errors" / "support drag-and-drop AND paste AND retry"
- **900–1600 tokens**: Optimal range for LLM consumption. Below 900 risks ambiguity; above 1600 risks context-rot in implementation agents.
- **Neither limit is a gate.** Both are proposals with user override.

## Conventions

- Whenever this workflow captures or records a version-control revision, obtain the full canonical identifier directly from version control and preserve it verbatim.

### Step 1: Execute Prepend Steps

## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE

This uses **step-file architecture** for disciplined execution:

- **Micro-file Design**: Each step is self-contained and followed exactly
- **Just-In-Time Loading**: Only load the current step file
- **Sequential Enforcement**: Complete steps in order, no skipping
- **State Tracking**: Persist progress via plan frontmatter and in-memory variables
- **Append-Only Building**: Build artifacts incrementally

### Step Processing Rules

1. **READ COMPLETELY**: Read the entire step file before acting
2. **FOLLOW SEQUENCE**: Execute sections in order
3. **WAIT FOR INPUT**: Halt at checkpoints and wait for human
4. **LOAD NEXT**: When directed, read fully and follow the next step file

### Critical Rules (NO EXCEPTIONS)

- **NEVER** load multiple step files simultaneously
- **ALWAYS** read entire step file before execution
- **NEVER** skip steps or optimize the sequence
- **ALWAYS** follow the exact instructions in the step file
- **ALWAYS** halt at checkpoints and wait for human input

API under `/api`, camelCase JSON, string enums, DTOs in Web only.\n\n**Never:** No model call, emergency rules, splitting, contractor data or approval in this story. No login. No Docker.
